營收走勢圖

研究員/蔡佾璋 EASONTSAI@uni-psg.com

儒鴻 （1476）9 月營收 35.1 億元，+6%MoM，+8.3%YoY，在船期持續緊
張，仍有 4.5 億營收遞延至 10 月情況下，9 月仍創單月歷史新高紀
錄，淡季不淡，3Q26 季營收達成率 102.5%，符合預期，故研究部持
續推薦儒鴻（1476），主要考量:（1）產能滿載，目前訂單能見度達
2027 年3-4 月，3Q26 GM 將優於2Q26，4Q26 ASP 優於 3Q26，公司於
紡織展期間上修 4Q26 營收為季增，2026 年營收將明確呈現季季高，
對 4Q26 至 1H27 展望樂觀。（2）公司以高強度研發能量將中國同業
殺價競爭擋在防火牆外，開發動能維持高檔，具競爭力的高毛利新品
佔比達 15-20%。（3）Wellness 新趨勢需求強勢崛起，Wellness 重視
親膚性與多場景穿著功能，有利於儒鴻產品定位，儒鴻視 Wellness
為台灣紡織業的新機會，公司產品開發方向緊扣此趨勢，營收佔比已
從 2025 年的 20%提升至 1H26 的 30%左右，成長趨勢明確。 （4）依目
前 EPS 推算，2027 年發放之現金股利殖利率約 5.2%，具下檔保護。
研究部預估儒鴻 2026 年、2027 年稅後 EPS 24.38 元(原估 24.09 元)、
26.12 元(原估 25.33 元)， 分別+21.3%YoY、+7.1%YoY。 美伊戰爭讓市
場對於 2H26 消費需求擔憂延續，然儒鴻目前本益比僅約 12-13 倍，
位在過往評價區間 12-33 倍最下緣，股價位階偏低，下檔風險有限。
考量 2027 年獲利成長幅度雖然較低，但公司近期上修展望，故將本
益比調升至 14 倍，目標價 365 元（14xPER*2027EPS）。

重點摘要： 
1. 儒鴻為垂直整合成衣大廠  
儒鴻為布料織造、染整加工到成衣生產的垂直整合紡織廠，
主要產品為功能性針織布料與成衣。儒鴻本身布料的研發能
力強，對新、 舊客戶來說都能提供最符合潮流 、主流產品， 核
心競爭能力強，公司持續致力成為該領域的市場領先者。

針織布產線共四座廠房，分別為台灣的溪州廠、大山廠及大
園廠，產能合計為800萬碼/月；另有越南廠，產能為700萬碼
/月，總產能合計共1500萬碼/月。成衣自有產線主要位於越
南 、 印尼及柬埔寨 ， 平均產能為840萬件/月 ， 加上外包的策略
聯盟生產基地約505萬件/月，總產能達1,345萬件/月。

儒鴻最主要的客戶為Nike，其2025年營收佔比為16.4%，UA佔
比約9.1%，Lululemon佔8.9%，以1H26來看，前三大及前五大
客戶訂單加總呈現年增，具成長潛力的小巨人們目前合計占
比約10%，全年目標小巨人營收占比可達12-18%。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

儒鴻的成衣客戶有50個以上，布料客戶有150-200個，客戶充
分分散，公司持續培養小客戶，螞蟻雄兵持續貢獻營收 。

2. 儒鴻布料內轉比例提高，推動成衣營收持續增長 
儒鴻2026 年前3 季 成 衣 及 針 織 布 營 收 比 重 分 別 為69.1% 及
30.9%，成衣佔比較去年同期+3.5pctYoY。

圖 1、2026 年前 7 月產品營收比重

成衣方面，2026年前3季營收209.1億元，+11.1%YoY。3Q26營
收70.9億元，-0.2%QoQ，+16.8%YoY。2Q26大量內轉的針織布
進行成衣加工後 ， 於3Q26反映在成衣營收上 ，3Q26的7月、9月
成衣營收皆創下歷史同期新高表現。新品牌客戶開發順利，
新客戶貢獻年增雙位數，既有客戶亦有個位數穩定成長。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

針織布方面，2026年前3季營收93.7億元，-4.6%YoY。3Q26營
收34.8億元 ，+16.8%MoM，YoY持平 。 針織布累積營收呈現微幅
下降，主因是公司貫徹垂直整合戰略，策略性提高自家針織
布供應給成衣部門使用的內轉比例主所致，針織布實際總生
產量與產能利用狀況並未衰退，仍維持年增表現。近五年針
織布外部銷售佔總營收比重自38%降至30%，布料內轉比例則
由25-30%大幅提升至40-50%。

3. 印尼三期擴廠計畫浮現 
印尼一 、 二期整體產能200~240萬件/月， 可貢獻總產值20億台
幣/年，滿編可達112條 ，目前產能全數開出， 稼動率滿載。 為
因應整體市場需求，公司規劃第三期工廠定位為高度自動化
廠區，未來若訂單持續成長並填滿新廠產能，高自動化所帶
來的邊際效益將會非常顯著。

新廠目前處於設計階段，產能規劃每月約200萬件上下。投產
初期會先以相對熟悉、 入門的產品進行試產。 訂單來源方面 ，
一部分會由越南廠轉單，一部分由品牌客戶直接下單。因規
劃導入更完善的自動化生產與管理系統，整體設計時程較原
先預期稍微延誤，管理團隊目前仍將量產目標設定在 4Q27開
始有實質產出。

由於印尼一、二廠目前產能滿載，公司正持續建置自有衛星
工廠以擴充產能，外包協力廠目前約占整體產能的27-28%。

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

4. 預估儒鴻 3Q26稅後EPS  6.06元，+7%QoQ，+0.6%YoY 
儒鴻2Q26營收100.9億元，+4.8%QoQ，+3.8%YoY，為16個季度
以來新高，毛利率30%，-2.2pctQoQ，+4.4pctYoY，季減主因
為部分低毛利長青款產品客戶下單力道比公司預期更強，且
部分產品開始使用到較高價原料所致。費用率10.8%，略高於
預期，主因員工年度調薪所致，營業利益率降至19.2%，營業
利益19.3億元，-11.9%QoQ，+28.9%YoY，稅後EPS 5.66元，-
15.4%QoQ，+158.3%YoY。

儒鴻3Q26營收105.7億元，+4.8%QoQ，+9.7%YoY，3Q26達成率
102.5%，符合預期。在船期持續緊張，仍有4.5億營收遞延至
10月情況下，9月仍創單月歷史新高紀錄，淡季不淡。公司表
示產能滿載 ， 上修4Q26營收為季增 ，2026年營收將明確呈現季
季高，並對4Q26至1H27展望樂觀，研究部預估費用率維持穩
定，營業利益率 19.8% ，稅後EPS 6.06 元(原估5.91元)，
+7%QoQ，+0.6%YoY。

5. 預估儒鴻2026年、2027年稅後EPS 24.3 8、26.12元，分別為
+21.3%YoY、+7.1%YoY 
儒鴻目前產能滿載 ， 且需加班因應 ， 因此公司對2H26營運看法
為審慎樂觀 ，2026年單月營收皆可維持30億元以上 ， 毛利率維
持28-32%區間 。 研究部預估2026年營收409.9億元(原估404.7
億元)，+7.9%YoY，漲價後的原料於6、7月開始使用，但透過
長期合作與多元供應商策略，成本上漲幅度在可控範圍內，
預估全年毛利率為30.4%，+1.7pctYoY，營益率將+1.5ppts至
20.2%， 營業利益82.6億元(原估81.6億元)，+16.5%YoY， 稅後
EPS 24.38元(原估24.09元)，+21.3%YoY。

目前訂單能見度達2027年3-4月 ， 儒鴻對1H27營運看法仍維持
審慎樂觀 ， 研究部預估2027年營收453億元(原估441.7億元)，
+10.5%YoY， 毛利率29.8%，-0.6pctYoY。 全年營業利益88.1億
元(原估85.9億元)，+6.6%YoY， 本業營運持續提升 ， 稅後淨利
71.7億元(原估69.5億元)，+7.1%YoY，EPS 26.12 元(原估
25.33元)。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

## Document 013: 2026-09-30_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ_ef0b0461

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-09-30_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ_ef0b0461.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ 
～不動産不況は脱却の兆しなお見えず～

調査部 主任エコノミスト 月岡直樹  
080-1069-6684 naoki.tsukioka@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 中国政府が新規則を発表。住宅は予約販売から完成後販売にシフト 
中国政府は8月28日、住宅販売制度と住宅ローン管理に関する新規則を発表した。新規の住宅不動産
プロジェクトについて完成後販売の方式を促すほか、未完成物件の予約販売における住宅ローンの実
行時期を物件引き渡し後に変更する。住宅の予約販売制度が不動産ディベロッパーの過度なレバレッ
ジ追求による資金繰り悪化と、それに伴う住宅引き渡し遅延問題を引き起こしたことから、住宅販売
モデルの転換を加速させる。 
住宅都市農村建設部や中国人民銀行、国家金融監督管理総局などの関連省庁が一斉に通達や管理規
定を公布した。住宅販売制度に関する通達では、①予約販売の代金（購入者が支払う頭金と銀行から
振り込まれる住宅ローン）について住宅不動産プロジェクトごとの専用口座を通じた銀行側による資
金管理を強化すること、②新たに不動産用地や建設許可を取得するプロジェクトに対して「完成物件
での販売を優先的に選択」させ、建設許可を取得済みの新規プロジェクトについても完成物件での販
売を奨励すること、③住宅不動産プロジェクトごとに会社を設立し、メインバンクを選定して開発資
金の融資を受けること、などの方針を示した。一方、住宅ローンの新たな管理規定には、予約販売に
おける住宅ローンについて「厳格にプロジェクト竣工届出後に実行しなければならない」と明記した
ほか、これまで最長30年とされてきた住宅ローンの借入期間を40年に変更することを盛り込んだ。 
■ 「保交房」に一定のメド。ディベロッパーの法的整理が進む可能性も 
中国の不動産市場はこれまで、ディベロッパーが物件の竣工を待たずに販売を開始する予約販売方
式が一般的であり、住宅ローンも購入者が予約購入した時点で実行され、返済義務が生じる慣行とな
っていた。予約販売には、購入者にとって不動産価格の値上がり前に物件を確保できるメリットが、
ディベロッパーにとって不動産開発に投じた資金を早期に回収できるメリットがあった。 
しかし、財務レバレッジの追求による収益の最大化を狙うディベロッパーは、回収した予約販売代
金を新たな不動産開発用地の獲得や不動産とは関係のないビジネスへの投資に流用し、事業を急拡大
させた。その実態は自転車操業であり、市場の過熱を懸念した中国政府が2021年にディベロッパーへ
の資金調達規制 （いわゆる 「3つのレッドライン」 ） を正式導入するとたちまち資金繰りに行き詰まり、
社債の債務不履行（デフォルト）が頻発する。ディベロッパーの信用力が低下したところにゼロコロ
2026 年 9 月 30 日

### Page 2

2

ナ政策の強化が追い打ちをかける形で販売が急減し、ディベロッパーの資金繰りが一段と悪化したこ
とで建設工事の進捗が滞り、2022年に入って予約販売済み物件の引き渡し遅延が深刻化する。 
ここに至って予約販売のデメリットが明らかとなる。すなわち、物件引き渡しリスクを負担するの
は購入者であり、たとえ遅延しても住宅ローンの返済を続けなければならないことである。事態を重
く見た中国政府は、「保交楼（予約販売済み未完成物件の引き渡し保証。「保交房」も同じ）」のス
ローガンを掲げ、ディベロッパーの資金繰り支援に乗り出す。2024年1月には、当局が融資適格と判断
した不動産開発プロジェクトをまとめたホワイトリストを作成し、各銀行がこのリストに基づいてデ
ィベロッパーに資金を融通するスキームを構築して、問題の解消を急がせたのである。 
中国政府がこのタイミングで新規則を発表した背景には、この「保交房」に一定のメドが付いたと
判断したことがあるとみられる。事実、国務院の李強首相は2026年3月の全人代における政府活動報告
で「『保交房』の任務は全面的に完成した」と宣言している。また、見逃せない動きとして、広東省
広州市の中級人民法院（地方裁判所）が8月21日、実質的に経営破綻している某ディベロッパーについ
て、債権者による清算の申し立てを受理したことが挙げられる。 
デフォルトを起こした不動産ディベロッパーの多くはこれまで、倒産によって債務の返済や予約販
売物件の引き渡し、建設業者への代金支払いなどの義務・責任を放棄することが許されず、債権者か
ら債務の減免や返済猶予の合意を得つつゾンビ化した状態で経営を続けてきた。ディベロッパーの破
綻が相次げば、不良債権問題の深刻化によって金融システムが動揺するリスクを招くだけでなく、予
約販売物件の引き渡しが困難となって社会問題に発展したり、下請け業者の連鎖倒産で景気が急激に
冷え込む可能性もあったからである。 
今回の動きは、清算の申し立てを裁判所が「受理」しただけではあり、実際に清算が進むのかはな
お予断を許さないが、中国政府がディベロッパーの法的整理を前に進める意思を示したとみることも
できる。今後、清算手続きが順調に進むのか、他のディベロッパーについても同様の動きが広がるの
かを注意深く見守る必要があろう。 
■ 住宅販売モデル転換の社会的意義は大きいが、不動産市況への影響は軽微 
では、住宅販売モデルの転換は不動産市場にどのような影響を与えるであろうか。その前にまず、
不動産市場の現状を整理しておきたい。 
資金調達規制の強化をきっかけとした不動産不況は6年目に突入した。中国の不動産市場規模（販売
金額）は2021～25年にかけて半分に縮小し、販売面積や開発投資額は4割、新規着工面積に至っては7
割も減少した（図表1）。2026年も不況脱却の兆しは見えず、1～8月の販売面積は前年比▲12.1％、開
発投資額は同▲19.9％と低迷が続いている。開発投資の先行指標とされる不動産開発向け銀行貸出も
同▲33.3％となっており、ディベロッパーにとって資金繰りが厳しい状況は変わっていない。 
販売が大きく落ち込んだことで、市場には過剰な在庫が積み上がっている。みずほ総合研究所調査
部の試算によれば、建設中の物件を含む中国の住宅在庫（商用およびオフィス用を除く）は2025年末
時点で40.8億平米に達する（図表2）。2025年の住宅販売面積が7.3億平米であったことから、新規着工
がゼロになると仮定しても在庫をすべて消化するのに5年以上かかる計算である。中国政府は2024年5
月、地方政府による住宅在庫の買い取り策を発表し、同年10月には地方政府が専項債（特別債）の発
行で得た資金を買い取りに使うことも容認したが、在庫の消化は遅々として進んでいない。

### Page 3

3

住宅価格（主要70都市平均）は2021年7月のピーク時から新築で▲14％、中古で▲23％下落し、全体
として下落傾向に歯止めがかかっていない。ただ、図表3のとおり、底堅い需要のある大都市（一級都
市：北京、上海、広州、深圳）と膨大な在庫を抱える地方都市（二級都市：各省の省都。三級都市：
省都以下の主要都市）で二極化が顕著になっている。特に上海は堅調で、その新築住宅価格は2021年
7月から＋24％も上昇している。とはいえ、その堅調さは資産としての不動産保有の魅力を有する一部
の都市に限ったものであり、市況の回復が地方都市にまで広く波及する様子はうかがえない。 
住宅引き渡し遅延問題の発生以降、引き渡しリスクのない完成物件の購入が増加しつつあるが、予
約販売は2025年時点でも依然として住宅販売面積全体の6割超を占めている（図表4）。住宅販売モデ
ルの転換、すなわち予約販売から完成後販売への移行と予約販売における住宅ローン実行の後ろ倒し
は、住宅購入者の物件引き渡しリスクを大幅に軽減することになり、消費者保護の観点からもその社
会的意義は非常に大きいといえる。しかし、これに伴ってディベロッパーの資金繰りはより厳しいも
のとなる。ディベロッパーの資金調達に占める販売代金（頭金などの前受金と住宅ローン）の比率は
4割を超えているからである（図表5）。このため、短期的には新規の開発（土地の取得や新規着工）

が減少する可能性が高い。ただ、新規則は完成

ッパーの法的整理、不良債権の処理を着実に進
め、市場を健全な状態に戻すことが不可欠とい

とみられる。

え、ノンバンクとの相互連関を通じた金融システム全体への波及にも警鐘

欧州：EU・中国はハイブリッド車の輸入制限で暫定合意。同様の措置は他の品目に広がる可能性
11
 EUと中国は、貿易不均衡の是正措置について暫定合意。中国からのハイブリッド車輸入は従来見通しの半分以下に
―EUが中国産EVに追加関税を賦課した2024年以降、追加関税の対象とならないハイブリッド車輸入が急増
― 欧州委員会の試算では、今回の措置によって2030年までの4年間で数百万台の輸入を削減見込み。直近では、EUが輸
入するハイブリッド車の約半分を中国車が占めており、域内自動車市場に一定のインパクト
 欧州委員会は、幅広い産業で貿易不均衡を是正する必要性を示唆。同様の措置が他の品目にも広がる可能性
― 欧州委員会のモニタリング対象（輸入増加と価格下落が併存する品目）は、中国からの輸入品が最多

 フィリピンでAZEC（閣僚会合）とAEBFが同時期に開催。ASEANのエネルギー安全保障に関心が高まる

―ASEANはエネルギーを化石燃料に依存。中東情勢も踏まえ、エネルギー調達安定化の重要性が高まる状況

 ASEAN5では石油の国家備蓄制度や設備が未整備。石油の安定供給に向けた対応の強化が急務

日本：コメ価格下落で企業物価は鈍化。もっとも、先行きは原油高を背景に再加速へ
18
 9月の国内企業物価はコメ価格の下落を主因に、前年比＋6.8％（前月：同＋7.6％）と鈍化
― 新米価格が昨年を下回ったこと等を背景に、コメ価格が大幅に下落。国内企業物価を▲0.75%Pt押し下げ
― 一方、3月来の原油高を背景に化学・プラスチック製品は高止まり。また、AI需要等を背景に非鉄金属も高い伸びを維持
 7月以降の原油高再燃を背景に、先行きの国内企業物価は再加速する見込み
― 原油価格高止まりにタンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの輸入物価は原油関連を中心に加速へ
― 国内企業物価は石油・石炭製品やラグを伴いつつ化学・プラスチック製品などを中心に再加速する見込み

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 澳洲 8 月就業人數勁增 3.95 萬人大幅優於預期，但勞動參與率上升至 67.1%，
推升失業率至 4.6%，呈現勞動力擴增但市場邊際降溫跡象。澳洲央行總裁布拉
克日前指出，失業率回升至5%有助於抑制頑固通膨。考量物價長期高於目標中
點，市場預期澳洲央行本週二將重啟緊縮，升息 1 碼至 4.60%，創 2011年底以
來新高。
 瑞士央行（SNB）上週四宣布連續第五次將關鍵政策利率維持在0%不變，符合
市場預期。儘管受油價推升使 8 月通膨微升至 0.8%，但仍處於目標區間，央行
預期中期通膨壓力溫和，現行極度寬鬆立場依然適當。決議公布後瑞郎兌美元跌
至 1.2077。相較全球主要央行相繼緊縮，瑞士央行維持超低利率，策略轉為視情
況調節匯市以穩定瑞郎。
 標普全球公布歐元區 9 月綜合 PMI 初值升至 53.1，創三年新高，製造與服務業
同步擴張，新訂單增速達四年多最快，印證經濟無懼美伊衝突引發的能源成本飆

升，展現超預期韌性。受能源價格傳導加速影響，通膨壓力再度升溫，儘管尚未
浮現薪資二輪效應，但強勁景氣數據促使市場大幅提高歐洲央行提前於10月例
會再度升息的押注。
 美國 9 月綜合 PMI 初值勁升至 58.4，創 62 個月新高；製造業與服務業 PMI 亦
逆勢走強至 57與 58.7，凸顯企業擴張強勁，引發市場對通膨持續性的擔憂。市
場焦點轉向 9 月 30日公布的美國 8 月 PCE、個人消費支出與 Q2 GDP終值，同
日日本亦將發布短觀調查與央行意見摘要。若終端消費延續強勁，將牽動美日央
行政策利率預期。

 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.655，低點在 31.866，以 31.790作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 2.8分。 
上週台幣先升後貶，周初，台股創新高，外資連續五日買超台股並匯入，推升
台幣最高來到 31.655，不過逢低吸引進口商、政府基金等買盤進場，台幣主要在
31.700 附近震盪；臨近周末，美國 9 月綜合 PMI 由 8 月的 56 走升至 58.4，創下逾
5 年來的新高，加上美伊戰事持續，皆推升市場對後續升息的預期，美元指數升至
101 上方，台幣跳空開低，回貶至 31.800 上方，最低來到 31.866，不過隨後出口商
賣壓出籠，台幣跌勢放緩，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.790。 
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國經濟表現仍具韌性，9月綜合
PMI 由 8 月的 56 上升至 58.4，企業活動加速至逾 5 年來最強，數據公布後美國 10
年債券殖利率升破 5%，Fed Watch 顯示市場對於聯準會 10 月升息預期升高，有助
於美元指數維持偏強格局，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，台股在創下
新高後回落，而外資也結束連續五日買超的走勢，轉為賣超，短線上資金匯入不利
台幣表現，然而，台灣8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6億美元、年增 71.4%，創歷史新高，
AI 需求帶動出口持續強勁，出口商後續拋匯需求預計將提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，
預估本週台幣區間震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走貶。人民幣中間價在上周連續十天走升，並升破6.7500整數
關口，最高來到 6.7468，帶動人民幣偏升，不過由於美伊戰事持續，市場對於聯準
會升息預期提升，美元指數震盪走強，加上週間公布美國9月綜合 PMI由 8 月的 56
走升至 58.4，創下逾 5 年來的新高，美元指數突破 101 整數關卡，令境外人民幣承
壓，USD/CNH回貶至 6.7200上方，終場境外人民幣收在6.7227，週線貶值283點，
境內人民幣則收在 6.7125，週線貶值 141 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣高檔震盪。就國際市場來看，美中兩國完成新一輪談判，美方
表示雙方同意將原訂 11 月 10 日到期的貿易休戰延長至明年 1 月 10 日，並持續就
關稅、貿易及供應鏈等議題進行協商，降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險，對人民幣形
成一定支撐。不過，美國近期經濟數據仍具韌性，加以聯準會偏鷹立場推升美元，
預計將限制人民幣升值空間；就中國國內市場來看，近期人行持續偏強設定中間價，
帶動人民幣維持偏強走勢，惟美中利差再度擴大，預計將限制人民幣升值的空間；
最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖中，匯價反彈至 20日均線之上，顯示原先下
行動能暫歇，上方壓力先看向季線 6.7350附近，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣高檔震
盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7400

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY先上後下。週初，美國多位聯準會官員鷹派談話，帶動美元指數
持續上行，推升 USD/JPY延續前一週的走勢來到 157上方；週間，受美國 PMI 強
於預期與美債殖利率全面上行帶動，市場重新提高對聯準會後續升息路徑的押注，
帶動 USD/JPY大漲突破 159的關卡；臨近週末，日本首相高市早苗表態日圓被低估
是個問題，使市場提高對政策訊號與潛在干預的敏感度，帶動USD/JPY回吐大部分
漲幅，終場收在 157.26，週線上漲 56點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本財務大臣片山皋月上週
五罕見透露，美國總統川普在美日元首峰會上對日圓過度疲弱表達關切，首相高市
早苗亦回應日圓遭低估確屬問題。儘管近期美元指數維持在 101 上方，但在日本政
府可能干預的陰影下，預計將限縮日圓貶值的空間。二、美國9 月綜合 PMI產出指
數創逾五年新高，疊加美伊戰事推升油價突破100美元與柴油大漲，通膨壓力劇增。
美聯儲理事巴爾表態可能需再升息兩次，芝加哥聯儲古爾斯比也示警能源衝擊具持
續性，推升市場押注 10 月升息機率升至六成，預計將支撐 USD/JPY 走勢。三、就
技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY連日上漲一度站回年線但隨即失守，目前依舊承
壓於季線及年線下方，不過各天期均線趨平，且下方尚有月線支撐，短線尚無明確
的方向。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，歐元延續前一週偏弱的走勢，加上聯準會官員鷹
派發言，導致歐元跌破 1.1450的重要關卡；週間，儘管油價自高檔回落，但市場解
讀為能源成本仍處高位、難以迅速回落，推升對美國實質利率與利差優勢的預期。
加上歐洲在能源依賴與成長動能較弱下，相對於美國更難承受長期高利率環境，歐
元最低來到 1.1358；臨近週末，歐元跌深反彈，終場收在1.1391，週線下跌 91點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學家連
恩示警，第二波能源價格飆升將推升通膨，預計至2027年中期始回落至目標水準。
德國央行月報指出，受中東衝突、低天然氣庫存及明年醫療改革影響，通膨將居高
不下；夏季雖因出口疲弱與乾旱致增長趨緩，但在製造業回暖與財政支出挹注下，
估年底經濟有望重啟復甦，預計將提供歐元一定的支撐。二、美聯儲官員接連釋放
強烈鷹派信號，聖路易聯儲總裁穆薩萊姆直言當前 3.75%-4.00%利率仍偏寬鬆，呼
籲盡早收緊；芝加哥聯儲總裁古爾斯比亦警告，AI投資繁榮引發需求過熱，疊加持
續性供應衝擊，可能迫使美聯儲加速且更積極地升息。目前Fedwatch顯示預期明年
4 月前可能再升息三次，將不利歐元表現。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
在各天期均線下方，且接連失守1.15及 1.14整數關卡，空方動能較佔優勢。綜合以
上，預估 EUR/USD本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.21-115.09.24 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.804% ~ 0.830%  
30天期短票利率   1.65% ~ 1.69%  
公債－10 年期-A15109     1.95%  
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.84%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本周央行持續每日減發定存單，令市場資金充裕，帶動短
率靠向區間低位。 
週二央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.638%，略低於
市場預期，且較上次標售結果下跌 2.5個基本點。 
週四隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.804%~0.830%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.65%~1.69%，利率較上週下跌 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109 及 5 年期指標券 A15108 均未成
交，週四等殖系統參考報價中價分別為 1.95%、1.84%。 
台股排擠效應、國際債市走勢偏空，加上油價續處高檔，通膨潛在威脅仍存，
12 月升息可能性未能完全排除，市場氣氛續偏觀望，預期台債殖利率將維持
高檔區間盤整 ， 預估5年券區間為 1.84%~1.90%，10年券區間為 1.95%~2.0%。

## Document 087: 261009_daiwa_asia-foundry

Source PDF: all report pdf/261009_daiwa_asia-foundry.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 44
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Asia ex Japan Information Technology

What's new: TSMC at its series of results calls delivered not only 
escalated business guidance but its effort of fab optimisation to address 
supply constraints driven by accelerated AI HPC demand. This, together 
with a ripple effect from advanced to specialty foundry demand spurred by 
AI scaling from compute to power and connectivity, will likely shift the 
paradigm in the industry, unlocking demand for specialty nodes which we 
expect to structurally benefit peers from 2H26. Among TSMC’s peers we 
cover offering specialty foundry works, we initiate coverage of Vanguard 
International Semiconductor (VIS) with an Outperform (2) rating and 12M 
TP of TWD210, as we believe VIS is benefiting from this paradigm shift.

What's the impact: AI scaling spurring specialty foundry demand. 
Flagged in our series of AI thematic reports since 2023, we envision the AI 
buildout a multi-year cycle scaling from generative to agentic to physical, 
and from cloud to edge to ubiquitous. The scaling is not only spurring 
demand for core chips benefiting advanced foundries but companion chips 
benefiting specialty foundries, in order to facilitate the entire ecosystem of 
compute, connectivity, and power. Since many AI companion chips do not 
require advanced nodes, second-tier foundries have followed TSMC as the 
second-wave beneficiary on the AI ramp, seeing improved capacity 
utilisation with improved pricing power, per our market research, thanks to 
a ripple effect in the specialty foundry demand which should help push 
utilisation higher into 2027 to reach full levels.

Augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation. We expect such a ripple effect 
to be further augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation, in order to address a 
supply shortfall in the advanced foundries caused by the strong AI HPC 
demand. This looks set to shift the paradigm in the foundry industry, 
structurally benefiting peers’ operating specialty nodes. We expect the fab 
optimisation to unlock business worth of c.USD3bn pa, or c.40% of UMC’s 
2025 revenue, with spillover taking effect from this year and ramping into 
2027-28E, considering different timings of TSMC’s fab closures and 
associated lead times for customer qualifications of alternative fabs.

What we recommend: Among the specialty foundries under our coverage 
in Asia, we initiate on VIS with an Outperform (2) rating and expect it to 
benefit from this paradigm shift, in addition to UMC (Hold [3]). HHGrace 
(Buy [1]) and SMIC (Sell [5]) should also benefit, yet to a lesser extent for 
the latter due to geopolitical issues.

How we differ: We believe we are one of few brokers quantifying business 
potential from TSMC’s fab optimisation to the second-tier foundries.

Asia Foundry

A paradigm shift in specialty nodes

➢ A ripple effect in demand from advanced to specialty foundries 
spurred by AI scaling from compute to power/connectivity 
➢ Likely paradigm shift in the foundry industry driven by TSMC’s fab 
optimisation augmenting demand for specialty nodes 
➢ Reaffirming our Positive stance on the sector and initiating coverage 
of VIS, which we expect to benefit from this paradigm shift

9 October 2026

Key stock calls

### Page 2

A paradigm shift in specialty nodes 
TSMC at its series of quarterly results calls this year (Too good to be true? (II), 16 April; 
Yes, it’s good and it’s true, 16 July) delivered not only escalated business guidance but its 
strategy of fab optimisation, in order to address supply constraints driven by rapidly 
growing demand for AI high-performance compute (HPC) chips that require advanced 
technology nodes. We view its fab optimisation as likely shifting the paradigm in the 
foundry industry, unlocking demand for specialty process nodes structurally benefiting 
peers from this year. TSMC’s fab optimisation should help augment a ripple effect in 
demand being spurred by AI scaling from compute to power and connectivity, ever since 
this multi-year AI buildout cycle began from 2023 after OpenAI’s ChatGPT dropped from 
the air.

Among TSMC’s peers we cover offering specialty foundry works, we see Vanguard 
International Semiconductor (VIS) as benefiting from this paradigm shift, thus initiating our 
coverage of the stock with an Outperform (2) rating and 12M TP of TWD210. Elsewhere, 
we believe UMC will benefit from this shift, thus raising our forecasts in this report, though 
we are comfortable with our Hold (3) rating on valuation grounds. While Huahong Grace 
Semiconductor (HHGrace; rated Buy [1]) stands to benefit from China’s AI developments, 
we see SMIC (rated Sell [5]) also benefiting, but to a lesser extent, due to geopolitical 
issues such as the US’s export controls prolonging its advanced technology developments.

AI scaling spurring specialty foundry demand 
As flagged in our series of AI thematic reports since 2023 (Let’s talk about AI or … bubble? 
11 July 2023, Let’s talk about AI, not bubble, 25 October 2024, and Let’s talk more about 
AI, ASIC AI this time, 1 July 2025), we have envisioned the AI buildout a multi-year cycle 
scaling vertically by language from generative (data training) to agentic (data inference) to 
ultimately physical AI, and scaling horizontally by application from cloud to edge to 
ultimately ubiquitous AI (ie, AI everywhere).

Ever since late-2022 when OpenAI’s ChatGPT dropped from the air, followed by Nvidia 
kicking off the AI build from 2023, this multi-year AI scaling has not only spurred demand 
for core chips such as GPU and application specific IC (ASIC) as accelerators benefiting 
advanced foundries, but for companion chips such as optical engines, drivers, super 
transimpedance amplifier (TIA), digital signal processor (DSP), and power semiconductors 
benefiting specialty foundries, in order to facilitate the entire AI ecosystem of compute, 
connectivity, and power, as well as memory, illustrated in a chart next page.

Many AI companion chips do not require advanced process nodes but specialty 
technologies such as silicon-germanium (SiGe), silicon-on-insulator (SoI), and bipolar-
CMOS-DMOS (BCD) with a variety of heavily doped wafer materials such as gallium-
nitride (GaN), GaN-on-silicon and GaN-on-silicon-carbide (SiC) handling a variety of 
different voltage currents ranging from a few volts to over 1,000v. Therefore, we have seen 
improved capacity utilisation rates (CUR) across the second-tier foundries since the start of 
the year which offer these specialty technologies, following TSMC as the second-wave 
beneficiary on the AI ramps, thanks to a ripple effect in the specialty foundry demand which 
should help push the second-tier CUR higher through 2027 to reach full levels, according 
to our market research.

As illustrated in a chart next page, we expect the CUR among the second-tier foundries of 
UMC, HHGrace, and VIS, following the trough in 1Q23 on the post-COVID inventory 
correction, to follow TSMC’s trajectory, accelerating in 2H26E to 90% or above and 
reaching the 100% level through most time of 2027-28E, resulting in improved pricing 
power for the like-to-like price hikes varied in different timings.

Augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation 
We expect such a ripple effect in the specialty foundry demand to be further augmented by 
TSMC’s fab optimisation, in which the foundry leader aims to address a supply shortfall in 
the advanced foundry works caused by the strong AI HPC demand, including AI CPU, all 
types of xPU as accelerators and base dies for high bandwidth memory (HBM), by TSMC’s 
definition. We expect its optimisation strategy to likely shift the paradigm in the foundry 
industry structurally to benefit peers operating specialty process nodes.

TSMC: expected schedule of mature fab closures 
Wafer fab Location Wafer size Process node Capacity (wpm*) Note 
Fab 2 Hsinchu Science Park 6" 0.5-0.8u 85,000  
Fab 5 Hsinchu Science Park 8" 0.15-0.35u 50,000  
Subtotal (8" equivalent)   97,813 40%+ of VIS revenue

Fab 15/P3 Taichung Science Park 12" 28/22/20nm 50,000 c.30% of UMC revenue 
Total (12" equivalent)     93,472  
Source: Company, Daiwa estimates 
Note: * wpm = wafers per month

According to our market research, in order to accommodate more advanced packaging 
capacity such as chip-on-wafer-on-substrate (CoWoS), TSMC is shutting down one 6” fab 
(Fab 2) and one 8” fab (Fab 5) this year with a combined capacity of c.98k wafers per 
month (wpm) in 8” equivalent size (ie, 200mm in wafer diameter). Covering process nodes 
ranging from 0.8u to 0.15u, closures of the 2 mature fabs will likely unlock business 
ranging from USD700m to USD800m pa, depending on the average selling prices (ASP) 
for different nodes ranging from USD250 for 0.8u to more than USD700 for 0.15u, on our 
estimates, or more than 40% of VIS’s 2025 revenue. We see VIS as the key beneficiary 
from this fab optimisation, given its strategic ties with TSMC.

We further expect TSMC to close one 12” mega-fab in Taichung Science Park named Fab 
15/P3 and convert it to accommodate more advanced capacity of N3, in order to meet the 
strong AI HPC demand. This mega-fab consists 2 phases thus operating a combined 
capacity of c.50k wpm in 300mm size, on our estimates, with process nodes mainly 
focused on 28/22/20nm for ASP ranging from USD3,800 to USD4,000. Therefore, the 
closure will likely unlock business of USD2.3bn pa on average, or c.30% of UMC’s 2025 
revenue. UMC and GlobalFoundries (GF) look well positioned to benefit here as both 
operate the 28/22nm process nodes. All together, we reckon the foundry leader’s fab 
optimisation will likely unlock extra business worth of USD3bn pa, or nearly 40% of UMC’s 
2025 revenue, with likely an order spillover taking effect from this year and ramping into 
2027-28, considering TSMC’s different timings of fab closures and associated lead times 
for customers to qualify alternative fabs which should typically take 9-12 months.

Stock recommendations 
Among the specialty foundries under our coverage, we expect VIS to benefit from this 
likely paradigm shift thanks to its historical ties with TSMC which has strategically helped 
transform VIS’s business from memory to mixed-signal processes and further advance it to 
value-add power semiconductors capitalising on the multi-year AI scaling. UMC stands to 
benefit from this scaling with its specialty foundry offerings and active 28/22nm ramps, 
though we view investors largely pricing in this potential. We expect HHGrace and SMIC to 
benefit from this AI scaling, but less than their Taiwan counterparts especially for SMIC, 
due to the geopolitical issues such as export controls and AI regulations from the US 
diluting the scaling benefit as the world appears to be divided into “China for China” and 
“non-China for non-China”, in terms of the semiconductor supply chain.

Key downside risks to our Positive stance on the sector are “silicon inflation” in the supply 
chain and geopolitical overhangs (war, tariff, interest rate) leading to global inflation and AI 
capex cuts. Please refer to the company section for more company-specific details.

We initiate coverage of Vanguard International Semiconductor (VIS) with an 
Outperform (2) rating and 12M TP of TWD210 by applying our ROE-adjusted PBR target 
of 5.2x one-year forward BVPS forecast. Key downside risks: 1) current chip upcycle ends 
earlier than expected; and 2) the 12” fab ramp/execution turns out below expectation.

We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating for TSMC and 12M TP of TWD3,000, based on an 
unchanged ROE-adjusted PBR target of 7.7x on our one-year forward BVPS. Key 
downside risks are silicon inflation and geopolitical overhangs leading to global inflation 
and AI capex cuts.

risk to our call would be any positive breakthrough on geopolitics and export controls.

### Page 8

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We concur what the chairman said on the 2Q26 results call 
that TSMC doesn’t sell milk (ie, a commodity), which one can buy at 
another convenience store. Yet when AI demand is deriving from its leading 
advanced services vs. the ‘milk’ one can buy from other convenience 
stores, peers stand to benefit from a ripple effect in demand on the AI 
scaling (ie, AI spillover), from core compute to memory to connectivity to 
power, augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation addressed in the main 
section of this sector report. That said, as TSMC still under-ships demand 
in the advanced foundry and backend services, we see a good chance for it 
to exercise pricing power in 2027, striking a balance between profitability 
and supply-chain interests. We haven’t embedded price hikes in our model, 
thus keep our forecasts and TP, reaffirming our Buy (1) call.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 preview and 4Q26 outlook. We estimate 
TSMC to have earned 3Q26 EPS of c.TWD28.5, varying not much from the 
consensus estimate. Monthly revenue came in at TWD982bn (+49% YoY) 
QTD, reaching 67% of our 3Q26E revenue which we consider as tracking 
inline. We expect TSMC’s 4Q26 revenue to advance 10% QoQ (+54% 
YoY) to USD50.4bn for an EPS of TWD31.8, also varying not much from 
the consensus. While we keep our 2027-28 forecasts unchanged, we see 
upside from a possibility that TSMC would raise wafer prices to mitigate 
cost pressure from its Arizona operation and materials cost hike across the 
supply chain. TSMC is scheduled to report on 15 October; we will offer our 
updates in due course.

Optimisation benefiting supply chain. As addressed in the main sector 
section of this report, TSMC is closing some of its legacy fabs over 2026-27 
to address the supply constraint caused by the strong AI demand for 
advanced frontend wafer manufacturing and backend packaging & testing 
technologies. We expect its fab optimisation to unlock businesses worth 
c.USD3bn pa and benefit peers who offer specialty foundry services. We 
expect the optimisation to augment a ripple effect in the AI demand on 
scaling from compute to connectivity to power (AI spillover). We believe 
TSMC is happy to let go such spillover businesses given its limited 
bandwidth to handle the advanced foundry and backend demand.

What we recommend: We stick with our 12M TP of TWD3,000 for TSMC, 
based on an unchanged ROE-adjusted PBR target of 7.7x on our one-year 
forward BVPS. We maintain Buy (1) with downside risks of silicon inflation 
and geopolitical overhangs leading to global inflation and AI capex cuts.

How we differ: Our 2028E EPS looks 15% below Bloomberg consensus 
likely due to our view calling for a soft-landing in the chip cycle.

Company profile 
Incorporated in Taiwan in 1987, Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co. (TSMC) is the world’s largest 
complementary metal oxide semiconductor (CMOS) foundry in revenue terms. TSMC offers foundry 
services such as wafer masking, fabrication, probing, packaging and testing, to a high variety of 
customers including fabless chipmakers and IDMs. Its manufacturing fabs are located in Taiwan, China, 
the US and Singapore.

Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing (2330 TT): 9 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 8 Oct 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company 
ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
TSMC's board of directors (BoD) has 10 members, including 6 independent directors
representing 60% of total directors. The background of TSMC's BoD is diversified, with members
from industries, academia, and law. Also, its board members include representatives from not
only Taiwan but also Europe and the US, bringing their experience in operating international
businesses to the board.
We believe the high percentage of independent directors and a diverse mix of expertise from the
board members strengthen the functionality of its BoD, as evidenced by TSMC's leading position
in the foundry market.
Capital management 1
TSMC pays its dividend on a quarterly basis. In the past 5 years, it has managed its dividend
payout well, as evidenced by its dividend in dollar terms, where it has never lowered its dividend.
As the leader in advanced technology nodes, TSMC has kept expanding its capacity for
advanced nodes; as such, we expect its capital intensity to increase to the level near or above
40% over 2025-26 (vs. its long-term target of mid-30%). However, we believe TSMC will at least
sustain its cash dividend payout in dollar terms over 2025-26E. We see its balance between
investment and dividend as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 1
TSMC’s sales to related parties remained insignificant, accounting for less than 1% of total
revenue in recent years. Purchases from related parties also represented less than 1% of total
cost of goods sold. Given the limited contribution of related-party transactions to TSMC’s
revenue and procurement base, we continue to view related-party exposure and concentration
risk as minimal
S Supply chainmanagement 1
TSMC continues to strengthen its supply chain management through sustainability risk
management and supply chain resilience initiatives. In 2024, 100% of tier-1 suppliers completed
sustainability self-assessments, while 100% of targeted critical suppliers completed third-party
RBA audits. The Company also maintained its responsible mineral sourcing program and
enhanced human rights oversight across its supply chain. We believe these efforts help reduce
supply chain and ESG-related risks
E Water & wastewatermanagement 2
TSMC continues to invest in water reclamation and conservation technologies to enhance water
security. The Company has integrated reclaimed water into its manufacturing process and is
expanding alternative water sourcing through partnerships with local governments. Together with
ongoing investments in water recycling and efficiency improvement, we believe TSMC is well
positioned to mitigate water supply risks and strengthen the sustainability of its operations
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
TSMC has maintained a waste recycling rate above 95% and kept landfill disposal at below 1%
for many years. The Company continues to reduce waste intensity through process
improvements and resource recovery initiatives. In 2024, TSMC established the semiconductor
industry's first Zero Waste Manufacturing Center to further enhance circular economy practices
and maximize resource utilization. We view TSMC's waste management performance as best-in-
class and see limited environmental risk from waste disposal.
E GHG emissions 1
TSMC continues to advance its net-zero strategy through renewable energy procurement and
carbon reduction initiatives. All overseas fabs and offices achieved net zero emissions in 2024,
while the Company expanded its contracted renewable energy capacity to 4 GW. TSMC
maintains its commitment to achieve net zero emissions by 2050, with intermediate targets of
60% renewable electricity usage by 2030 and 100% by 2040. We view TSMC's climate strategy
as best-in-class among global semiconductor manufacturers
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 13

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Given its longstanding position in the global foundry industry, 
ever challenging TSMC’s leadership during the late 1990s, we believe 
UMC stands well to benefit from a ripple effect in demand spurred by AI 
scaling, as detailed in the main section of this report. We expect UMC’s 
wafer loadings to reach 100% from 1Q27, with price hikes to go through 
(see Price hike to go through? Still likely, 31 July). That said, we believe the 
equity market has priced in this AI strength on extra volume and pricing 
power for UMC. Hence, we stick with our Hold (3) rating on the counter.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 preview and 4Q26 outlook. We expect UMC to 
report 3Q26 net profit of TWD16.4bn (+9% YoY), varying not much from the 
Bloomberg consensus. Its monthly revenue finished at c.TWD49bn QTD, 
reaching 65% of our/consensus 3Q26 estimates, which we consider 
tracking inline. For 4Q26, we expect UMC to earn net profit of TWD16bn, 
13% below consensus, as the street seems to be assuming higher non-
operating gains. UMC is scheduled to report results on 28 October; we will 
be offering updates in due course.

<=28nm ramp on AI scaling and fab optimisation. UMC’s 28/22nm-and-
below revenue contribution has hovered around the high-30% range over 
the past 2 years, after meaningful ramp during the COVID period on 
broadened demand applications from display drivers and TV SoCs to 
power management. With AI scaling creating a ripple effect in companion 
chip demand for connectivity (ie, silicon photonics) and power (ie, server 
PMICs), we believe UMC is well positioned to benefit from any spillover 
demand, with the strength augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation, which 
could strengthen UMC’s pricing power. Given that UMC’s wafer loadings 
are likely to reach 100% from 1Q27, we expect its price hikes to go through 
and model increases of 2-5%, effective from 1Q27, across nodes related to 
AI applications. This should resume its <=28nm ramp to exceed a half of 
revenue in 2028. All in, we raise our 2027-28E EPS by 22%.

What we recommend: Despite our forecast revisions, we remain 
comfortable with our Hold (3) rating for UMC, as we believe its AI strength 
is already priced in. We raise our 12M TP to TWD145 (previous: TWD108), 
based on our higher ROE-adjusted PBR target of 4.2x (previous: 3.3x) 
applied to 1-year-forward BVPS on a higher ROE trajectory. Upside/ 
downside risks would be any price hikes above our expectations/ 
geopolitical overhangs (war, FX, interest rate) weighing on end-demand.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS do not vary much from consensus, 
indicating that UMC’s AI strength is already reflected in its valuation.

9 October 2026

United Microelectronics

A beneficiary of the AI ripple effect, but …

➢ UMC looks set to benefit from AI demand spillover 
➢ Yet we believe the AI ripple effect is being priced in 
➢ Maintain our Hold (3) call, with a higher 12M TP of TWD145

Company profile 
Incorporated in Taiwan, United Microelectronics Corp (UMC) is one of the leading semiconductor 
foundries in the world and second largest in Taiwan. UMC offers a series of foundry services from 
photo-mask, wafer fabrication to wafer probe & test. Its wafer fabs are spread over Taiwan, China and 
Singapore, with technology offerings ranging from 0.25u and above, 0.18/0.15u, 0.13u, 90nm, 65nm, 
45/40nm, 28/22nm to 14nm FF.

### Page 17

United Microelectronics (2303 TT): 9 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 8 Oct 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company 
ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
UMC’s Board of Directors consists of nine members whose expertise covers industry, academia,
finance, accounting, and management. Among these directors, six are independent directors,
representing 67% of total directors in 2024. The percentage of independent directors is high
relative to the regulatory requirement of at least 20% or at least 2 independent directors.
Although some of UMC's executive management are also directors, UMC's Chairman and
President are not the same person. Considering this and its relative-high percentage of
independent directors, we believe the board can perform its functionalities well
Capital management 2 It kept its dividend payout ratios above 50% in 2017-25, which is at a healthy, sustainable level.Overall, we see the balance between investment and dividend as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 1 Sales to related parties were around 1-2% of its total revenue each year. The percentage isinsignificant and thus we see limited risk for UMC.
S Supply chainmanagement 2
UMC has constructed an evaluation framework for reviewing the sustainable development of its
supply chains based on UMC’s GG-026-004-C sustainable supply-chain management review
guidelines to ensure its suppliers fulfil environmental protection, social responsibility, labour and
human rights, safety & health and sustainable development guidelines. We believe these efforts
could reduce its supply-chain risk.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 2
UMC developed its “Water Management Policy and Commitment” in 2015 to promote more
effective utilisation of water resources. In line with this policy, UMC continues to enhance
recycling through increased use of reclaimed and desalinated water, ongoing wastewater
treatment R&D, and optimised discharge management. In 2024, the company achieved a 30.3%
reduction in water withdrawal per unit product, surpassing its 14% goal and meeting its 2030
target ahead of schedule, which demonstrates UMC’s strong commitment to water conservation.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
2
To achieve the goal of zero pollution, UMC has established a comprehensive new material
evaluation process to effectively screen and control new materials introduced into the
development of new processes. Through this effort, it can avoid or reduce the use of raw
materials and the associated environmental impact. UMC was the first semiconductor
manufacturer company in the world to obtain QC-080000 IECQ Hazardous Substance Process
Management (HSPM) certification for all its fabs by leading global semiconductor manufacturers
in completing the third-party verification and audit of its HSPM system in 2006.
E GHG emissions 2
UMC was the first semiconductor company in Taiwan to pass integrated environmental
protection, safety and health management system certifications in 2001. UMC has established
the UMC Low Carbon Commitment as a guide for future carbon reduction projects. Its GHG
emission reduction goals include: 1) to reduce fluorinated greenhouse gas emissions intensity by
65% over 2013-25 vs. 2010, and 2) to reduce electricity consumption intensity by 15% in 10
years over 2016-25 vs. 2015.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 18

China Information Technology

What’s new: As China’s foundry leader, SMIC should benefit, among local 
peers, from China’s AI innovations to cope with geopolitical pressures, such 
as multiple lithography patterning, time scaling in advanced packaging 
technology and logic folding. Yet, to serve what we view as China’s 
“national mission” to catch up with the West in AI, SMIC has to suboptimise 
its operations for suboptimal profitability, as evidenced by its abnormal 
capex cycle and resulting depreciation burden. As such, we remain 
comfortable with our Sell (5) rating on the stock, though we view such a 
rating in capital markets may not be fair and truly reflecting its authentic 
fundamentals.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 preview and 4Q26 outlook. We expect SMIC to 
report 3Q26 net profit of USD399m, 7% below consensus, as the street 
appears to be assuming higher non-operating gains. For 4Q26, we forecast 
net profit of USD364m, down 9% QoQ but up 40% YoY, on flat top-line of 
USD3.1bn (+25% YoY), due to seasonality, as customers tend to manage 
inventory towards year-end.

Geopolitical concerns lingering despite AI. As China’s foundry leader, 
SMIC should benefit, among local peers, from China’s innovative AI 
developments, such as multiple lithography patterning, time scaling in 
advanced packaging technology and logic folding. Yet, to serve what we 
view as China’s “national mission” to catch up with the West in AI, SMIC 
has to suboptimise its operations for suboptimal profitability, as evidenced 
by its abnormal capex cycle over 2020-26 and the resulting depreciation 
burden. This, combined with lingering geopolitical uncertainty, prevents us 
from turning positive on the stock. President Xi and President Trump met in 
the US last month; we hope that any constructive breakthroughs in the 
future will help reset SMIC’s competitiveness for normalised profitability.

What we recommend: We rate SMIC Sell (5), with a 12M TP of HKD40 
(unchanged), based on our ROE-adjusted PBR target of 1.1x applied to our 
one-year-forward BVPS forecast. Though our rating may not truly reflect 
SMIC’s fundamentals due to geopolitical issues, we await any 
breakthrough between the 2 power nations on trade conflicts to normalise 
SMIC’s operations. Key upside risk to our call would be any positive 
breakthrough on geopolitics and export controls.

How we differ: Our 2028E EPS is 4% below Bloomberg consensus, likely 
due to our view calling for a soft-landing in the chip cycle.

9 October 2026

Semiconductor Manufacturing Int'l Corp

Yes, AI, yet constrained by geopolitics

Company profile 
Incorporated in China in 2000, Semiconductor Manufacturing International Corp (SMIC) is the largest 
dedicated semiconductor foundry in China. It offers product services, such as wafer fabrication, 
masking, packaging and testing. Its fab operations spread over major cities of China, including 
Shanghai (8", 12"), Beijing (12"), Tianjin (8”) and Shenzhen (8", 12"). It has also set up JV fabs with the 
governments and teamed up with Jiangsu Changjiang Electronics Technology (JCET, 江蘇長電).

Semiconductor Manufacturing Int'l Corp (981 HK): 9 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 7 Oct 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company 
ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
SMIC's Board of Directors (BoD) has 9 members in 2025, including 1 executive director, 4 non-
executive directors and 4 independent directors. Its BoD members have a diverse background,
and SMIC's chairman and CEO are not the same person.
Capital management 3
SMIC's capex in 2025 was approximately USD8.4bn. Management expects capex in 2026 to
maintain roughly flat to slightly down compared to 2025, which exceeded 10% of the Company’s
latest audited net assets, with large capital needs.
Related party &
transaction 1
SMIC has established a comprehensive supplier management framework covering supplier
onboarding, periodic evaluation, quality audits and ESG compliance, which helps mitigate
potential related-party transaction and procurement risks. Suppliers are assessed through a
cross-functional process covering quality, cost, delivery, service, environmental and compliance
factors, while higher-risk suppliers are subject to enhanced monitoring and corrective actions.
SMIC also emphasizes supplier diversification to reduce geographic and concentration risks,
with raw material suppliers distributed across China (71.3%), Eurasia (22.9%) and the Americas
(5.8%) in 2025. Overall, the Company's structured supplier evaluation and geographic
diversification mechanisms provide a reasonable level of control over supply-chain and related-
party transaction risks.
S Supply chainmanagement 2
SMIC continuously improves the specification of layered management for suppliers. The
specification covers the whole life cycle of the supply chain and refines specific requirements for
access, evaluation and management. Incorporating multiple aspects including strategic supply
chain management, and raw materials and spare parts procurement, SMIC has established
global supplychain management and planning, to ensure product quality and service capabilities
of supply chain. The company supports the development of the local supply chain and aims to
increase the proportion of its raw material suppliers based in mainland China annually. By
increasing local supply, it can reduce its procurement cost and thus increase its operational
efficiency and also partially mitigate the geopolitical risks, in our view.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 2
The company has implemented water-saving projects to improve water usage and reduce
external water source consumption. These projects include reducing water consumption in the
facility system, increasing wastewater reclamation, improving its water production rate and
lowering water draining losses.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
2 It has implemented cleaner production projects to optimise its raw materials usage, reduceresource consumption, and minimise waste generation at the source of production.
E GHG emissions 1
SMIC is continuously implementing energy conservation and emission reduction projects. Every
year, SMIC promotes energy-saving and emission reduction projects in an orderly manner so as
to improve energy efficiency, reduce GHG emissions, and address climate change.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 23

China Information Technology

What’s new: We expect HHGrace to continue to post positive earnings 
growth momentum in 3Q26E and 4Q26E, on the back of both volume and 
ASP strength. We believe HHGrace will continue to inject Huali’s (HLMC) 
assets into the listed company, thereby expanding HHGrace’s business 
portfolio and manufacturing footprint.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 preview and 4Q26 outlook. We forecast 
HHgrace to report revenue of USD775m in 3Q26E, up 22% YoY and 8% 
QoQ, driven by 15% YoY volume growth and a 6% YoY hike in ASPs. On a 
QoQ basis, we estimate a 3% improvement in ASPs. As a result, we expect 
net profit of USD58m in 3Q26E, up 124% YoY and 49% QoQ. We forecast 
the growth momentum to sustain into 4Q26, with 22% YoY expansion in 
revenue to USD804m, and 282% YoY growth in net profit to USD67m.

Mainstream business to benefit from AI ripple effects and TSMC’s fab 
optimisation. HHGrace focuses on the production of Nor Flash, MCUs, 
power discretes, analog and RF products, covering 55nm and above 
process-node technology platforms. HHGrace began raising ASPs in 4Q25, 
thanks to demand tailwinds from AI investment. Also, we expect TSMC’s 
capacity optimisation to cause short-term supply disruptions, resulting in 
sustained strength in pricing power for legacy-node foundry players.

Steady manufacturing footprint expansion. Despite wafer supply 
tightness, HHgrace maintains its Fab 9 expansion timeline, with wafer 
loading at Fab 9A (83,000wpm capacity) in 3Q26, reaching full utilisation in 
2H27. Furthermore, Fab 9B (55,000wpm) is set to kick-start small-volume 
production in 2027, per HHgrace. We believe the expansion focus will be 
on the 55/40/28nm process nodes. HHGrace plans to complete HLMC’s 
Fab 5 asset acquisition in 2H26. We expect more asset injections in 2027.

What we recommend: We maintain our SOTP-based 12-month TP of 
HKD139. We believe HHGrace will inject HLMC’s assets into the listed 
company by 2030. We factor in the future asset value of HLMC through to 
2030 by applying 2.5-4.0x PBRs to the fabs in Kangqiao, Zhangjiang and 
Chengdu, as well as HHSemi’s assets, and then discounting back by 3.5 
years at a 10% rate. We then apply a 70% discount due to the potential 
dilution from share issuance related to the acquisition of HLMC’s assets. 
We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating. Key downside risk: changes in asset-
injection plans.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 9-30% below the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely as we are more cautious about its ASP and gross margin 
outlook.

### Page 28

Taiwan Information Technology

Investment case: We initiate coverage of Vanguard International 
Semiconductor (VIS), a leading specialty foundry, with an Outperform (2) 
rating and 12-month TP of TWD210. We expect VIS to enjoy fully loaded 
operations (in terms of wafer-in) through 2027 with pricing power, thanks to 
a ripple effect as AI demand scales from compute to power, further 
augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation. Given VIS’s historical ties with 
TSMC, which have strategically helped it advance its business 
transformation towards value-added specialty foundry offerings, it looks 
well positioned to benefit from this multi-year AI scaling, cemented by the 
fast expansion of its first 12” fab in Singapore to capitalise on what we 
observe as a paradigm shift in the foundry industry.

Fully loaded operations on AI ripple effect. We expect VIS’s wafer 
loadings to approach full levels in terms of wafer-in through 2027, thanks to 
the AI scaling out and across creating a ripple effect for demand spillover 
and benefitting specialty foundries. Given its strategic partnership with 
TSMC, VIS stands to benefit from this multi-year AI build. Despite its pull-in 
of the 12” ramp, we do not expect such fast expansion to dwarf utilisations 
since all the planned capacity is sold out, thanks to long-term agreements 
(LTA) signed with customers amid the supply constraint, which should help 
VIS manage surging depreciation and minimise margin dilution.

Fast expansion to capture the paradigm shift. To capitalise on the likely 
paradigm shift in the specialty foundry segment, as detailed in the sector 
section, driven by TSMC’s fab optimisation, VIS is pulling its 12” fab 
construction in Singapore nearly half a year ahead of schedule. VIS aims to 
start mass production from 1Q27 after hosting an opening ceremony on 28 
September. We expect this fab to reach its first-phase designed capacity of 
44,000 wafers per month (wpm) by end-2028, with c.30% of it dedicated to 
TSMC’s wafer interposers and the rest for customers’ power semiconductor 
and embedded flash chips, mainly for AI applications. We forecast VIS to 
enjoy counter-seasonal quarters through 2027, delivering revenue CAGR 
of c.30% over 2026-28 for an EPS CAGR of 26%.

Catalysts: 1) Record-breaking monthly revenue run rates through 2027; 2) 
upbeat guidance for the coming quarters; and 3) margin resilience to ease 
investors’ concerns about depreciation amid the 12” ramp.

Valuation: We derive our 12M TP of TWD210 for VIS by applying our 
ROE-adjusted PBR target of 5.2x to our one-year-forward BVPS forecast.

Risks: 1) Current chip upcycle ends earlier than expected; and 2) the 12” 
fab ramp/execution turns out below expectation.

9 October 2026

Vanguard International Semiconductor

Initiation: fast ramp to capture the AI ripple effect

➢ Fully loaded through 2027 on AI scaling and fab optimisation 
➢ Fast expansion to capture a paradigm shift in the foundry sector

How do we justify our view? 
Growth outlook Valuation Earnings revisions

Growth outlook

VIS: quarterly EPS growth trend 
VIS’s earnings have been volatile due to the cyclical nature 
of the semiconductor industry in which it operates. That 
said, barring its hefty growth during 2021-22 as an outlier 
due to the COVID-inflated chip demand, VIS will enjoy 30-
70% YoY EPS growth over 2Q26E-4Q27E, according to 
our forecasts, with strength similar to that in 2016 and 
2018. On an annual EPS basis, we forecast a 30% CAGR 
over 2025-28.

Source: Company, Daiwa estimates and forecasts

Valuation   VIS: 4-quarter-forward PER bands 
Trading at 24x on our 4-quarter-forward EPS, the VIS stock 
is priced at the high end of its post-2009 GFC range of 5-
30x, suggesting a not-so-compelling valuation vs. history. 
Yet, considering our forecast of a robust 30% EPS CAGR 
over 2025-28, 24x does not look demanding from a PEG 
point of view. Valued by our ROE-adjusted PBR target of 
5.2x, our 12M TP of TWD210 for VIS translates into a 26x 
4-quarter forward PER, which looks reasonable in 
combination of both PBR and PER methodologies.

Source: Company, TEJ, Daiwa estimates and forecasts

Earnings revision

VIS: consensus EPS forecast revisions 
Bloomberg consensus 2026-27 EPS forecasts for VIS have 
been trending up since February 2026, indicating positive 
investor sentiment. Our 2027E EPS is 5% above 
consensus, implying room for further upward revisions from 
the market to act as an upside catalyst for the stock, in our 
opinion.

Company profile  
Founded in December 1994 and listed in March 1998, Vanguard International Semiconductor Corp. 
(VIS) successfully transformed its business model from memory to a complementary metal-oxide-
semiconductor (CMOS) logic foundry focused on specialty technologies such as mixed-signal, power 
analog and embedded memory, becoming one of the world’s leading specialty foundries. Leveraging its 
strategic relationship with major shareholder TSMC, VIS operates wafer fabs in Taiwan and Singapore, 
serving a wide variety of customers, including fabless chipmakers and IDMs globally.

### Page 32

32

Vanguard International Semiconductor (5347 TT): 9 October 2026 
Fast ramp to capture AI ripple effect 
We initiate coverage of VIS with an Outperform (2) rating and 12M TP of TWD210. 
Founded in December 1994, headquartered in Hsinchu Science Park and listed in March 
1998 (see Appendix 1 for more details on the company), VIS looks set to enjoy fully loaded 
operations (in terms of wafer-in) through 2027, with sustained pricing power, thanks to the 
ripple effect of AI demand scaling from compute to connectivity and power, further 
augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation.

Given its longstanding ties with TSMC, which currently holds a c.19% stake in VIS and has 
strategically helped it advance its business transformation by moving up value verticals of 
specialty-foundry offerings to differentiate itself in the competitive specialty foundry market, 
VIS looks set to benefit from this multi-year AI scaling, among peers. The fast expansion of 
its first 12” fab in Singapore should help cement the AI business and capitalise on what we 
observe as a paradigm shift in the foundry industry, as mentioned in the main section of 
this report.

Fully loaded operations on AI ripple effect 
Like many other semiconductor foundries, VIS enjoyed unusual pricing power during the 
COVID period as the virus, together with geopolitical tensions between China and the US, 
inflated chip demand across virtually all product applications to an unprecedented level. 
Consequently, the industry suffered from an unprecedented 2-year downturn on a 
prolonged inventory correction, if excluding the AI build that has helped rescue the 
advanced foundry segment, save the village (ie, the chip industry) and bring the global chip 
cycle back on its historical track of an average of a one-year downturn. This downturn 
caused choppy operations for many second-tier foundries which focused initially on the 
non-AI space over 2022-25, including VIS (see our series of Technology Outlook reports: 
2023: V-shape, U-shape or double dip?, 2024: The dragon is ready to soar, 2025: likely 
longer and stronger).

Starting from 2H25, after the AI builds scaled out and across from compute into memory to 
connectivity and power, creating a ripple effect in demand that benefits not only advanced 
but also specialty foundries, we expect VIS’s wafer loadings to approach full levels through 
2027 (in terms of wafer-in), despite its fast 12” expansion. With its parent company TSMC’s 
strategic partnership, VIS stands at a strong position to benefit from this multi-year AI 
scaling, in our view. To maximise the AI business potential, VIS is accelerating its first 12” 
fab in Singapore via a JV named VisionPower Semiconductor Manufacturing Corp 
(VSMC), with phase-one investment of USD6.8bn where, in terms of registered capital of 
USD4bn, VIS assumes USD2.4bn for a 60% stake, while NXP assumes USD1.6bn for a 
40% stake.

In terms of the total investment on a cash basis, VIS will chip in only USD1.0bn, with 
USD2.8bn coming from NXP and USD1.1bn from TSMC with some equipment 
consignment, while the rest will come from other customer prepayments, loans and a 
subsidy from the Singapore government. Upon VSMC’s completion, this will bring VIS a 
total of 6 fabs, with combined capacity reaching up to c.380,000 8”-equivalent wafers per 
month (wpm) by end-2028, based on our forecasts (see table on next page for details of 
our key business assumptions).

Fast expansion to capture the paradigm shift 
On top of the AI scaling creating a ripple effect for demand spillover, such strength looks to 
be further augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation, as assessed in the main section of this 
report. To capitalise on the likely paradigm shift in the specialty foundry segment by 
TSMC’s fab optimisation, VIS is pulling in its first 12” fab construction around half a year 
ahead of schedule, aiming to start mass production from 1Q27 after it hosted an opening 
ceremony on 28 September.

Registered under VSMC, we expect this fab to reach its first-phase designed capacity of 
44,000 wpm by end-2028, subject to market dynamics. c.30% of the fab’s capacity will be 
dedicated to TSMC’s wafer production of silicon interposers handling chip-on-wafer-on-
substrate (CoWoS) advanced packaging, which has seen meaningful supply constraint 
due to strong AI high-performance compute (HPC) demand. The rest of the capacity is for 
other customers’ power semiconductors (such as mixed-signal, analog and PMIC) and 
embedded flash, focusing on AI scaling and automotive and industrial (A&I) applications.

Despite the 12” expansion pull-in, we do not expect the ramp to dwarf capacity utilisations 
since all the planned capacity is already sold out, thanks to the long-term agreements 
(LTA) signed with customers amid the supply constraints, which should help VIS manage 
surging depreciation and minimise margin dilution for financial resilience. Although this 12” 
fab has little to do with TSMC’s 28/22/20nm fab optimisation addressed in the main 
section, since it covers only process nodes ranging from 0.13u to 40nm and thus enjoys 
only the 65/55nm wafer interposer spillover from TSMC, on our market research, we see 
possibilities for TSMC to shut down more legacy 12” fabs in the future. We believe this may 
incentivise VIS to speed up its second 12” fab construction in Singapore, namely the 
phase-two, or VSMC Fab 2, for 40,000-50,000 wpm capacity beyond 2028 and is scalable 
to 28nm-and-below and even the finFET (FF) process nodes.

Vanguard International Semiconductor (5347 TT): 9 October 2026 
Financial forecasts 
In the near term, we expect VIS to enjoy counter-seasonal quarters through 2027, thanks 
to the ripple effect of AI scaling on wafer loadings and pricing power. We estimate that VIS 
earned 3Q26 EPS of TWD1.57, in line with the Bloomberg consensus estimate. For 4Q26, 
we are behind consensus since 1) we expect a diminished effect of price hikes after our 
estimated 2-3% QoQ EPS increases in 3Q26; and 2) we do not expect the 12” contribution 
to ramp that soon. In the long term, we forecast VIS to deliver a revenue CAGR of nearly 
30% over 2026-28 for an EPS CAGR of 26%.

Revenue and customers 
As of 2Q26, power management IC (PMIC) accounted for the majority of revenue (77%; 
see chart on next page), on a rising trend from 40% a decade ago thanks to the co-efforts 
between VIS and TSMC strategically transforming the former’s business up the value 
verticals from logic commodity display drivers (D/D) for consumer electronics devices to 
power discrete, analog, and PMIC for not only consumer but A&I applications. Riding on 
the escalating trend of demand for advanced high-V PMIC accommodating AI servers and 
power infrastructure under the AI cycle, we expect the PMIC revenue contribution to rise 
further over our forecast period, while the D/D contribution will drop to c.10%-plus for base-
line customer demand support.

As of 2Q26, VIS found 16%, 16%, and 46% of revenue from major process nodes of 
0.25u, 0.18u, and 0.13u-and-below, respectively, on our estimates. Since the VSMC fab 
will focus on 0.13u all the way to 40nm, and TSMC’s wafer interposers will take c.30% of 
the capacity ramp on our estimates focusing on 65/55nm, we expect the 0.13u-and-below 
to increase to more than 60% of revenue over our forecast period. Both the PMIC and 
node advances should be blended ASP and margin accretive, helping mitigate the 
depreciation burden associated with the VSMC fab ramp. Illustrated in a chart on the next 
page, we expect VIS’s blended ASP to trend upward from c.USD630 in 2Q26, rising by 1-
5% QoQ through our forecast period to exceed USD700 by 2028.

VIS services a high variety of customers around the world, including most fabless 
chipmakers in Taiwan and mainland China focusing on the D/D, power MOSFET and 
PMIC, and most integrated device manufacturers (IDM) across Japan, Europe and the US 
focusing on power semiconductor foundry works.

Vanguard International Semiconductor (5347 TT): 9 October 2026 
Cost analysis 
Over the past decades, VIS’s cost structure has fluctuated in fixed overheads (mainly D&A) 
from time to time, depending on its fab acquisitions, varying from 10% to 30% of COGS 
(refer back to the key assumption table). Given VSMC is about to ramp in production with a 
total capex of USD6.8bn for phase one, or roughly 2x VIS’s 2025-26E aggregate revenue, 
we expect the D&A portion to return to the 30% level over our forecast period, acting as a 
margin dilutor. Yet with VIS’s capex budget of TWD60-70bn (c.USD2bn) this year, we 
expect the capex to largely subside from 2027 for the depreciation cycle to peak in 2029. 
As a result, we forecast its D&A in absolute terms to rise by 54% YoY in 2027 to TWD14bn 
and 33% YoY in 2028 to nearly TWD19bn, with gross margin (GM) stalling at 32-34% over 
2Q26-1Q28, then picking up to 35% or higher in 2H28 and likely rising further after the 
depreciation comes off the cycle into 2029.

Minority interest 
Minority interest (MI), or non-controlling interest as stated by VIS, kicked in from 1Q25 and 
rose to 2.4% of pretax income in 2Q26 due to preparation for the VSMC investment. We 
forecast the MI to rise to TWD341m this year (TWD137m in 2025), TWD568m in 2027 and 
TWD1.45bn in 2028, mainly from the 40% MI owned by NXP as VIS ramps the 12” fab 
investment in Singapore. As illustrated in a table below, we forecast VSMC to record 
revenue of nearly TWD9bn in 2027 and TWD26bn in 2028 for operating loss of TWD1.4bn 
and operating profit of TWD3.6bn, respectively, on the basis of a standalone operating 
entity.

Dividend policy 
Over the past decade, VIS has stepped up its dividend payout ratios from 50% in 2011 to 
more than 100% this year. We expect the company to keep the ratios at c.80% over our 
forecast period, subject to the AI development which should determine the capex 
requirement for the VSMC investment, affecting its FCF generating power. VIS paid a 
TWD4.5/share cash dividend this year (against last year’s earnings), and we forecast it to 
pay TWD5.0/share for 2027 with a payout ratio of 85% against this year’s earnings for a 
2.7% yield based on the closing price as of 5 October.

VIS: free cashflow trend     VIS: debt-to-equity ratio

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts  Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Cashflow and gearing 
Given its hefty capex over 2025-27E, we expect VIS’s free cashflow (FCF) to turn negative 
during the same period. Foreseeing the cash gap, VIS issued new shares of 228m in 2024 
for a 12% per-share dilution and collected TWD17.6bn. It further issued ECBs in February 
this year to collect nearly TWD11bn; therefore, we forecast its debt-to-equity (D/E) ratio to 
approach 170% in 2026. Yet with the VSMC ramp in profitability for operating cash inflow, 
we forecast the FCF to turn positive in 2028, helping lower the gearing to c.140%.

Valuation and risk 
We employ an ROE-adjusted PBR methodology to value the foundry stocks under our 
coverage, where we compare a foundry’s projected ROE with its historical ROE average, 
work out a premium or discount, and apply it to the foundry’s historical average PBR for 
our ROE-adjusted PBR target. Given TSMC is VIS’s strategic partner, we tweak our 
methodology a bit by anchoring VIS’s ROE-adjusted PBR to TSMC’s, where we compare 
their long-term ROE projections (2004-28E), work out a discount of 33% and then apply 
the discount to our 7.7x target PBR for TSMC, resulting in our target PBR of 5.2x and 12M 
TP of TWD210 for VIS on our one-year forward BVPS forecast.

Key downside risks to our call would be: 1) the current chip upcycle ends earlier than 
expected; and 2) VSMC’s 12” fab ramp/execution comes in below expectation.

Vanguard International Semiconductor (5347 TT): 9 October 2026 
Appendix: company profile 
Founded in December 1994 with headquarters located in Hsinchu Science Park and 
shares listed in March 1998, Vanguard International Semiconductor (VIS) is a leading 
specialty foundry in the world focusing on mix signal, power semiconductor and embedded 
memory chip manufacturing for value-added differentiation against competition in the 
mature foundry market. With strategic help from its parent company TSMC on business 
transformation and technology transfer, VIS commanded a 1.1% share in the global 
foundry market by 2025 revenue on our estimates (total size c.USD140bn), or nearly 4% in 
the global mature foundry market (ex-TSMC as a proxy), behind SMIC, UMC, 
GlobalFoundries (GF) and Huahong Grace Semiconductor (HHGrace; see chart on the 
next page). Note: our foundry market calculation is for dedicated foundry works only, 
different from TSMC’s revised definition of the Foundry 2.0 that includes not only the 
dedicated foundry but packaging, testing and other non-memory semiconductor 
manufacturing services.

The foundry market 
Ever since TSMC’s founder innovated the semiconductor foundry service model, the 
foundry market has been in operation for more than 3 decades. Thanks to a rise in the 
global AI buildout, the global foundry revenue, after a dip in 2023 on the post-COVID 
inventory correction, recovered by more than 20% YoY pa over 2024-25. We forecast the 
growth to accelerate to more than 30% YoY this year, with revenue reaching USD186bn 
and exceeding USD255bn in 2028 for a robust 23% CAGR over 2025-28. If excluding 
TSMC, we forecast the market to grow by 14% YoY this year and approach USD60bn in 
2028 for a CAGR of 13%, where we expect VIS to outgrow the market growth over our 
forecast period as illustrated in a chart below, thanks to its fast capacity expansion to 
capitalise on the AI scaling and paradigm shift in the specialty foundry segment.

Operation and customer 
VIS currently operates 5 wafer fabs with 4 in Taiwan, including Fab 1 which commenced 
operations in 1994 as a memory setup and then converted to mixed signal, power metal 
oxide semiconductor field effect transistor (MOSFET) and logic foundry fab; Fab 2 which 
was acquired in 2007 from Winbond; Fab 3 which was acquired from Nanya Tech in 2014; 
and Fab 5 which was acquired from AUO in 2021, plus 1 in Singapore, Fab VS1, acquired 
from GF in 2019 and previously named 3E. The 5 fabs currently operate a total of 280,000 
wpm capacity all in 8” wafer size, in which the Fab VS1 and 5 are scalable for an extra 
10,000 wpm capacity each, though VIS doesn’t seem to be keen in exercising the 
scalability since VSMC apparently is the focus for expansion. Following its opening 
ceremony on 28 September this year, VIS’s first 12” fab via VSMC is slated for mass-
production from 1Q27, aimed to bring up total capacity to c.380,000 wpm in 8” equivalent 
wafer by end-2028.

 Source: Bloomberg

VIS doesn’t disclose its key customers due to confidentiality issues, but we believe its key 
customers cover most of the fabless chipmakers in Taiwan and mainland China focusing 
on display driver (D/D), power MOSFET and power management IC (PMIC), as well as 
integrated device manufacturers (IDM) across Japan, Europe and the US. According to our 
industry survey, key D/D vendors in the greater China region are Novatek (3034 TT, 
TWD529, Hold [3]), Himax (HIMX US, not rated), Raydium (3592 TT, not rated), Ilitek (6962 
TT, not rated), Sitronix Technology (8016 TT, not rated), FocalTech (3545 TT, not rated), 
Chipone Technology (not listed), OmniVision (603501 CH, CNY82.21, Hold [3]) and 
GalaxyCore (688728 CH, not rated), while key power semiconductor vendors in the world 
include Qualcomm (QCOM US, USD177.12, Outperform [2]; covered by Louis Miscioscia), 
Renesas (6723 JP, JPY3,924, Outperform [2]; covered by Junji Okawa), Monolithic Power 
Systems (MPS; MPWR US, not rated), NXP (NXPI US, not rated), STMicro (STM US, not 
rated), Infineon (IFX GR, not rated), Microchip (MCHP US, not rated), TI (TXN US, not 
rated), MediaTek (2454 TT, TWD4,835, Buy [1], via Richtek), Global Mixed-mode 
Technology (GMT; 8081 TT, not rated) and Niko Semiconductor (3317 TT, not rated).

Shareholder structure 
VIS’s major shareholders were TSMC and the Taiwan government’s Development Funds, 
with c.19% and c.16% of holdings, respectively, as of August 2026 under total share 
outstanding of 1.88bn. With management in aggregate holding an insignificant stake and 
the rest being held by institutional investors (QFII holding was 32.7% as of 5 October), we 
estimate a healthy free float of more than 60% for the stock, with decent cap of c.USD11bn 
and 3-month daily turnover of more than USD100m.

TSMC reduced its holding in VIS this year by 8pp, raising investor concerns on whether 
the move would jeopardise the former’s strategic support to the latter. We see no direct link 
between the stake reduction and strategic support, and believe TSMC will continue the 
support, evidenced by its licence of gallium-nitride (GaN) and interposer technologies and 
equipment consignment to VSMC.

ESG analysis

Update Date: 8 Oct 2026 
Source:  Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
VIS's board of directors (BoD) consists of 9 members, including 5 independent directors,
representing 56% of the total board seats. The board comprises members with diverse
backgrounds in business management, finance, industry, and academia, providing a broad
range of expertise and perspectives.
We believe the majority-independent board structure and the directors’ collective experience in
leadership, corporate governance, industry expertise, financial management, risk management,
and sustainability strengthen the effectiveness of the BoD and support VIS's long-term growth
and corporate governance practices.
Capital management 1 VIS pays its dividend on a quarterly basis. In the past 5 years, it has managed its dividendpayout well, as evidenced by its dividend in dollar terms, where it has never lowered its dividend.
Related party &
transaction 1 Sales to related parties were around 1-2% of its total revenue each year. The percentage isinsignificant and thus we see limited risk for VIS.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
VIS has established a comprehensive sustainable supply chain management framework to
ensure suppliers comply with its sustainability requirements covering environmental protection,
labor and human rights, occupational health and safety, business ethics, and responsible
sourcing. The company requires suppliers to adhere to its "VIS Supplier Code of Conduct" and
"Corporate Sustainability Policy", and conducts supplier sustainability assessments and audits
as part of its supplier management process. We believe these efforts strengthen VIS's supply
chain resilience and help mitigate supply chain risks.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 2
VIS is committed to improving water efficiency and sustainable water management. In 2022, the
company implemented the ISO 46001 Water Efficiency Management System to analyze water
consumption patterns, identify significant water-use equipment, evaluate water-saving
opportunities, and establish improvement measures. VIS also provides annual water
management training at its Taiwan facilities to promote continuous improvement in water-use
efficiency. We believe these efforts demonstrate the company's commitment to responsible
water stewardship and help reduce operational risks associated with water resources.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
VIS adopts a comprehensive resource management approach to waste management and has
established waste management procedures in accordance with ISO 14001 standards. Through
dedicated waste management personnel and regular employee training, the company promotes
effective waste reduction, classification, collection, storage, and treatment practices across its
operations.
E GHG emissions 1
VIS adopts a source-segregation and multi-stage treatment approach, supported by high-
efficiency air pollution control systems, to effectively manage VOC and acidic/alkaline gas
emissions from semiconductor manufacturing processes.
The company's emission levels remain well below regulatory limits, reflecting its commitment to
environmental compliance and sustainable operations.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

1 
6274 台燿 
(Taiwan Union Technology Co.)

報告日期： 2026/10/08 研 究 員：方姿婷  (Lesley Fang) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要： 
 2Q26 營收符合預期， 毛利率受 1H26 漲價提升 ， 預期 3Q26 毛利
將順利反應成本上漲與優化之產品組合 ， 毛利率預估提升 3%+ 。  
 AI 伺服器帶動高階銅箔基板需求強勁，同時驅動高階網路交換
器升級動能，銅箔基板需要更高速傳輸、訊號低損耗之下會陸
續採用 CCL M8、M9 規格。 
 2H26 低軌 S 客戶 V3 版本新型火箭搭載低軌衛星，所採用之銅
箔基板升級至 M8 等級；以及，1.6T 光模組需求量增帶動銅箔
基板需求量增。

一. 2Q26 營收與財報符合市場預期，3Q26 營收優於預期  
台燿 2Q26 營收為 143.01 億元，+42.2%QoQ、+110.93%YoY，毛利
率為 29.72% ，稅後淨利為 23.42 億元，單季稅後 EPS+7.82 元。2Q26
台燿受到 ASIC CCL 專案出貨帶動，產品組合優化，毛利率已相較
1Q26 季增 4.5% 。台燿已公告 3Q26 營收為 179.48 億元(優於市場預
期 3%)，+25.50%QoQ，毛利率可順利轉嫁且高階 CCL 出貨比重提
升，預估台燿 3Q26 毛利率為 33.21%，稅後淨利 36.72 億元，單季
稅後 EPS+12.60 元。

二. AI 伺服器帶動 CCL/PCB 產值提升，產能擴充 33% 
宏遠投顧觀察，AI 伺服器與先進封裝等需求日增，高階 CCL 市場
需求將在 2024 年~2026 年 CAGR 達 26% ， 而高階 CCL 供給端 CAGR
則是 7%，高階市場需求殷切。高階銅箔基板(包含 AI 伺服器和高
階網路交換器，自 2024 年始採用 M8 CCL 材料；2026 年 M8 材料
佔總體 CCL 市場 40%，M8 材料佔比顯著高於 2022 年、2023 年的
22%和 26%。台系銅箔基板供應鏈當中，M6~M8 等高階材料的供
應廠商為(2383)台光電為首、(6274)台燿次之。回顧 3 年來，台燿
產能擴充進程，端視泰國廠進度而定。4Q26 泰國新廠產能陸續開
出，台燿總產能為 260 萬張/月，+13%YoY。展望 2027 年，台燿泰
國廠、常熟廠初步先提升 30 萬張/月，逐步提升總新增產能達 120
萬張/月，預估 2027 年底時，總產能達 380 萬張/月，+46%YoY；預
估 2028 年底時，台燿總產能擴充至 530 萬張/月，+39.5%YoY，平
均 3 年每年產能擴充約 33% 。高階 CCL 在 AI 伺服器、高速網路交
投資建議：買進

換器(高階 Switch)帶動之下，一旦，泰國廠產能擴充可望使得台燿
市佔率得以提升 。 2027 年隨著 NVIDIA Rubin 平台將帶動 CCL M8+ 、
M9 材料出貨攀升，AI ASIC CCL 拉貨動能呈現 50~60%高度成長，
同業台光電與台燿高階 CCL 新產能釋出 ， 產品組合持續優化 。 2027
年起 ， 除一般型伺服器 Intel Diamond Rapids 平台 、 AMD Zen6 Venice
平台採用 PCIe Gen6 與 Ultra Low Loss CCL(M7)，PCB 板層數增至

數 46~52L、玻纖 Low Dk2/Q 布、銅箔 HVLP4/5) ，預期 CCL 廠商產
品規格顯著升級。

三.  台燿高階網路 Switch 板材、低軌衛星板材、1.6T 光
模塊板材，27 年規格朝向高階型號發展 
展望 2H26，在高階 Switch 升級方面，800G Switch 採用 M8、M9 
CCL 規格，2H26 台燿將新增高階 Switch 客戶，隨著 AI 伺服器、
高階網路交換器產業發展朝向 800G、1.6T Switch 升級趨勢帶動之
下， 
銅箔基板需要更高速傳輸、訊號低損耗之下會陸續採用 CCL M8、
M9 規格。此外，2H26 開始 800G 與 1.6T 光纖收發模組(採用 M8、
M9 CCL)開始放量，AI 資料中心帶動光纖收發模組需求，400G 以
上光纖收發模組出貨量 2026 年 1 億個、2027 年 1.45 億個、2028 年
達到 1.6~1.65 億個；在中系系統廠商積極拉貨之下，1.6T 光纖收
發模組 2026/2027/2028 年估量 15~20M/55~60M/85~100M ， 1.6T 光纖
收發模組，於 2 年內呈倍數增長。低軌大型 S 客戶 V3 版本新型火
箭搭載低軌衛星 ， 所採用之銅箔基板升級至 M8 等級 ， 材料損耗要
求降低，訊號傳出完整性標準提高，對於 CCL、PCB(HDI)、玻纖
與銅箔等規格預期同步升級，推升材料成本與 PCB 產值。

四.預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 38.49/66.22 元 
2026 年受惠 AI 伺服器及 ASIC 需求持續強勁的帶動下，台燿為網
通、高階網路交換器銅箔基板重要供貨廠商，將受惠 AI Server 對
於高階銅箔基板強勁的需求，因而，預估台燿 2026 年營收 618.78
億元，+108.01%YoY，毛利率 31.13%，稅後淨利 114.79 億元，稅後
EPS+38.49元 。 隨泰國廠產能釋出 ， 預估台燿 2027年營收增至 963.61
億元，+55.73%YoY，毛利率為 33.26%，稅後淨利 198.23 億元，稅
後 EPS+66.22 元。

四.投資建議維持買進，目標價 2,150 元 
宏遠投顧認為，台燿營運重點在於，營運動能於 2026 年起加速啟
動，一般型伺服器 CPU 廠商 Intel 、AMD 均有新品帶動新平台升級
與相關供應的零組件商機，以及攻入 AI 伺服器、AI 高階網路交換

貳、公 司 概 況

一. 公司簡介 
台燿(6274)為銅箔基板廠商，主要產製雙面印刷電路板與多層印刷
電路板之銅箔基板 、 內層銅箔基板 、 黏合片 ， 產品應用別當中 ， ELL
與 SLL(M7、M8)佔比 39%、VLL 與 LL(M4、M6)佔 31%、HDI 與 N-
LL 佔 16%、其他品項佔 14%。

二.2026~2027 年營運概況及展望 
1.HDI 與 M8 以上升級仍為關注焦點，2026~2028 年產能建置積極 
過往一般型伺服器用板採用 HDI 設計，強調細線路設計，CPU 尺
寸變大 Pin Count 數量增加至 2,000 只~4,000 只 Pin Count，PCB 板
面積不變之下要走向細線路化(線寬/線距)朝向更細線路設計。製
程上 HDI 經過多次壓合後，PP 在熱壓多次後，每一層熱膨脹係數
不同，導致通孔 Crack 或翹曲，製作時容易產生爆板，因此對於
PCB 板廠製作良率會是一大技術難關，而金像電、健鼎等廠商於
高階 HDI 相對具備生產優勢與競爭力，相對應台光電、台燿等高
階 CCL 供貨與升級也成為製程一大瓶頸；回顧 3 年來，台燿產能
擴充進程，端視泰國廠進度而定。4Q26 泰國新廠產能陸續開出，
台 燿 總 產 能 為 260 萬 張 /月，+13%YoY。展 望 2027 年，台 燿 泰 國 廠、
常熟廠初步先提升 30 萬張/月，逐步提升總新增產能達 120 萬張/
月 ， 預估 2027 年底時 ， 總產能達 380 萬張/月 ， +46%YoY ； 預估 2028
年底時，台燿總產能擴充至 530 萬張/月，+39.5%YoY，平均 3 年
每年產能擴充約 33%。高階 CCL 在 AI 伺服器、高速網路交換器
(高階 Switch)帶動之下，一旦，泰國廠產能擴充可望使得台燿市佔
率得以提升。2027 年隨著 Nvidia Rubin 平台將帶動 CCL M8+、M9
材料出貨攀升，AI ASIC CCL 拉貨動能呈現 50~60%高度成長，台
光電與台燿等高階 CCL 新產能釋出，產品組合持續優化。

針對 AI GPU 與 ASIC 發展進程，Google TPU 出貨量從 2026 年 400
萬顆上修至 2027 年(V8 增量)增至 800 萬顆，倍數增長。AWS ASIC 
預計 2027 年出貨量達 330~350 萬顆，增長+21%YoY 。NVIDIA 體系
GPU，Rubin 系列層數提升至 24~26L，材料端從 M8/M8+往更高規
格升級，更高層數與多層式架構，促使 PCB 板訊號走線、供電完
整性、高速訊號之阻抗匹配控制、訊號傳輸完整性，皆帶動 PCB
